● Everything verified — IDwarfMapper, DwarfMapperFacade, AddDwarfMappers(), ValidateDwarfMaps(), DwarfMapperValidationRoot, plus an assembly-level [DwarfMapperDefaults] that solves per-class option
  repetition. Here's the design.

  Design: AutoMapper → DwarfMapper, Consumer

  1 — Branch & wiring
  Branch mapper-to-dwarf-transition off develop in ConsumerSolution. Cross-repo ProjectReference centralised in a new Directory.Build.props: a $(DwarfMapperRepoRoot) property plus an ItemGroup guarded by
  per-project <UseDwarfMapper>true</UseDwarfMapper>, emitting the runtime reference and the generator as OutputItemType="Analyzer" ReferenceOutputAssembly="false". Both are required per-project —
  DwarfMapper marks its analyzer PrivateAssets="all", so it does not flow transitively.

  2 — Call sites barely move
  IMapper → IDwarfMapper; all 693 .Map<T>(x) call sites stay verbatim. The 7 AddAutoMapper(typeof(XProfile)) become AddDwarfMappers().ValidateDwarfMaps(), and Consumer.Web (composition root) gets
  [assembly: DwarfMapperValidationRoot] so a missing provider is a DWARF061 build error, not a runtime throw.

  3 — Two non-obvious constraints driving the class layout
  - [DwarfMapper(...)] options are class-level, but AutoMapper's ForAllMembers was per-map. The 9 null-skip maps must be split into their own partial class carrying SkipNullSourceMembers = true.
  - DwarfMapper defaults to enum by-name; AutoMapper is by-value. One assembly-level [DwarfMapperDefaults(EnumStrategy = ByValue)] per project buys parity without repeating it 12 times. (Null collections
  need no change — Consumer never sets AllowNullCollections, so AutoMapper's null→empty already matches DwarfMapper's AsEmpty.)

  4 — Four hard cases, not find-and-replace

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                         Case                          │ Count │                                                    Resolution                                                    │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IncludeBase                                           │ 15    │ No primitive — restate shared [MapProperty]/[MapIgnore] on each derived method (script-generated to avoid drift) │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ConvertUsing(typeof(ConcurrentListToListConverter<>)) │ 1     │ Open generic is a non-goal — enumerate closed instantiations, one map each                                       │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ctx.Mapper inside ConstructUsing/AfterMap             │ 3     │                                                                                                                  │
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

● Feasibility looks good: .NET 10.0.101, Docker 29.6.1, and the API already exposes /health — exactly the readiness probe this needs. One caveat: ConnectionStrings:ConsumerDatabase is empty and the
  compose file has no Mongo service, so whether the hosts reach ApplicationStarted without your real infra is the single unknown. I've designed around that rather than assuming it.

  ---
  Design: AutoMapper → DwarfMapper in Consumer

  1. Branch & wiring

  Branch mapper-to-dwarf-transition off develop in ConsumerSolution. A new Directory.Build.props defines $(DwarfMapperRepoRoot) and, guarded by a per-project <UseDwarfMapper>true</UseDwarfMapper>, emits both
  references — the runtime assembly and the generator as OutputItemType="Analyzer" ReferenceOutputAssembly="false". Both are needed per-project: DwarfMapper marks its analyzer PrivateAssets="all", so it
  deliberately does not flow transitively.

  2. Call sites barely move

  IMapper → IDwarfMapper (ambient facade, same Map<T>(object) shape) — all 693 call sites stay verbatim. The 7 AddAutoMapper(typeof(XProfile)) become AddDwarfMappers().ValidateDwarfMaps(). Consumer.Web
  gets [assembly: DwarfMapperValidationRoot], turning "no provider for this pair" into a DWARF061 build error instead of a runtime throw.

  3. Two constraints that drive class layout

  - [DwarfMapper(...)] options are class-level; AutoMapper's ForAllMembers was per-map. The 9 null-skip maps get their own partial class with SkipNullSourceMembers = true.
  - DwarfMapper defaults to enum by-name, AutoMapper to by-value — a silent behaviour change if missed. One assembly-level [DwarfMapperDefaults(EnumStrategy = ByValue)] per project. Null collections need
  no change: Consumer never sets AllowNullCollections, so AutoMapper's null→empty already matches AsEmpty.

  4. Four hard cases

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬─────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                         Case                          │  N  │                                               Resolution                                                │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IncludeBase                                           │ 15  │ No primitive — restate shared config on each derived method, script-generated to prevent drift          │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ConvertUsing(typeof(ConcurrentListToListConverter<>)) │ 1   │ Open generic is an explicit non-goal — enumerate closed instantiations, one map each                    │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ctx.Mapper in ConstructUsing/AfterMap                 │ 3   │ No ResolutionContext — the mapper class calls its own generated methods directly from [AfterMap]        │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ CreateMap<ICollection<Rank>, UserRanksDocument>       │ 2   │ Collection→object isn't a mapping shape — hand-written Convert method (user methods win over synthesis) │
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Expect a DWARF001 wall on first build: members the profiles were silently dropping. Each gets an explicit decision (map / [MapValue] / [MapIgnore]), recorded in a ledger — this is the migration paying
  for itself.

  5. Before/after tests — three legs

  (a) Parity snapshots. New Consumer.Mapping.ParityTests. For each of ~300 directed pairs: Fuzzer.Generate<TSource>(n, seed) → map → snapshot. Committed on AutoMapper first, then re-asserted against
  DwarfMapper via GraphOracleComparer.CrossTypeDiff. Divergences require an explicit documented allowlist entry — never a silently-refreshed snapshot.

  (b) Round-trip. [RoundTrip] on the 94 reverse pairs, verified through RoundTrip.Verify.

  (c) Resource benchmark — your start/stop idea, two levels:

  - Level A (hermetic, no infra): build the container exactly as Program.cs does, resolve the mapping root, then map one instance of every pair. Measured in two phases, because AutoMapper 14 compiles maps
  lazily — config-build alone would undercount it and flatter AutoMapper. Phase 1 = config build + resolve; Phase 2 = first-map of every pair, the warm-up cliff DwarfMapper doesn't have. Reports wall
  time, GetTotalAllocatedBytes, heap, and loaded-assembly count (AutoMapper + System.Linq.Expressions machinery disappearing is a variance-free number).
  - Level B (full host, out-of-process): API and Web, Release, no debugger. Readiness = time-to-first-200 on /health; shutdown via a benchmark-only StopApplication() endpoint. Samples working set, private
  bytes, TotalProcessorTime, and I/O via a GetProcessIoCounters P/Invoke — System.Diagnostics.Process exposes no I/O counters on Windows. N≥10 runs, median + IQR.

  Both levels use one mapper-agnostic harness committed before conversion, so the same code produces both sides. Results land in benchmarks/results/YYYY-MM-DD-*.md, machine-stamped and never quoted
  unqualified.

  Honest caveat: the mapper delta in Level B may be small against Blazor, SignalR, OTel and Mongo startup. That's precisely why Level A exists — Level A isolates the change, Level B shows what a user
  feels.

  6. Sequencing

  Smoke-run Level B first (the boot unknown). Then profiles easiest→hardest: 4 trivial (Tertiary, Secondary, Primary, ChatHistory) → 3 small (Premium, Server, Chatters) → 2 medium (User, Admin) → 3 hard
  (Services/23 + IncludeBase, Repository/79 + hooks, Dtos/104 + open generic). AutoMapper is removed last, so the branch compiles at every commit.

  ---
  Two notes before I write this up as a spec:

  - If the hosts can't boot without your Mongo/OAuth secrets, Level B degrades to an in-process host-build measurement and I'll report that plainly rather than fabricate numbers. Do you have a local Mongo
  I should point appsettings.Benchmark.json at?
  - Unrelated, but I saw it while reading config: ConsumerApiOptions:JwtSecret is a real-looking signing secret committed in appsettings.json. Worth rotating if that's ever been a production value.

  Shall I proceed with this design?

● Studying the options surface paid off — it changed three things, including a parity divergence I'd have shipped as a silent behaviour change. But first, the tooling.

  Rider MCP isn't connected

  There's no JetBrains/Rider MCP server in this session — I checked. Available MCP servers are roslyn-lens, project-rag, context7, claude-in-chrome, and the claude.ai connectors. And roslyn-lens is pinned
  to DwarfMapper.NET.sln — it auto-discovers from the working directory, and refuses to switch:

  Path not in discovered solutions list.
  discovered: ["...\DwarfMapper.NET\DwarfMapper.NET.sln"]

  So I can't drive Rider or run Roslyn semantics over Consumer as things stand. Three ways forward, and I'd like you to pick:

  1. Install the JetBrains MCP server (Rider's MCP Server plugin + add it to .mcp.json) — genuinely the best fit for what you're describing: inspections, refactorings and run configurations driven from
  the IDE that already has the solution loaded.
  2. Re-point roslyn-lens at Consumer — it discovers by working directory, so a second instance scoped to ConsumerSolution (or launching from there) gives me get_diagnostics, find_references,
  get_type_hierarchy on the real solution. That covers most of what I'd want Rider for.
  3. project-rag index_codebase on ConsumerSolution — works right now, no setup, but it's semantic search, not compiler truth.

  Worth saying plainly: for the DWARF001 wall specifically, dotnet build is the authoritative source regardless of which of these we use. The MCP tooling speeds up navigation, not correctness.

  3. project-rag index_codebase on ConsumerSolution — works right now, no setup, but it's semantic search, not compiler truth.

  Worth saying plainly: for the DWARF001 wall specifically, dotnet build is the authoritative source regardless of which of these we use. The MCP tooling speeds up navigation, not correctness.

  What the options study changed

  A parity divergence I'd missed. NullStrategy defaults to Throw — a nullable-value source mapping to a non-nullable target throws, where AutoMapper silently substitutes default. That's a runtime
  exception waiting in production. Parity needs NullStrategy = SetDefault, joining EnumStrategy = ByValue in the assembly defaults. Exactly the class of thing that would have passed tests and failed live.

  The profile shape survives. I'd assumed ~300 named partial methods. Wrong — class-level pair-scoped attributes ([MapProperty<S,T>], [MapIgnore<T>], [MapValue<T>]) configure a [GenerateMap] pair with no
  method at all. So a Profile full of CreateMap().ForMember() becomes a class full of attributes, near 1:1. Much smaller diff than I described.

  ConstructUsing has a purpose-built answer: [MapConstructor<TSource,TTarget>(nameof(Factory))] — pair-scoped, names a factory on the mapper. Better than the hand-written Convert I proposed for those 9
  sites.

  One constraint confirmed rather than assumed: SkipNullSourceMembers lives in the policy layer (assembly or mapper only — not pair-scoped), so the 9 ForAllMembers maps genuinely do need their own partial
  class. That part of the design holds.

  Two opportunities the migration unlocks, worth a decision later, not now: AutoMatchMembers = false on inbound API DTO→entity maps is a compile-time mass-assignment guard, and RequiredMapping = Both
  would surface source members nothing reads.

  Infra verdict

  I checked, and the shared-Postgres risk is lower than I feared: nothing calls Database.Migrate() at startup — the only MigrateAsync is an admin-triggered chat-history endpoint. So repeated host starts
  won't mutate schema.

  I agree with you that full Docker isolation isn't worth the time here. Recommendation: skip it. Level A needs zero infra — it builds the container and maps every pair in-process, and that's where the
  clean mapper number lives anyway. Level B uses your local Mongo and the shared Postgres read-mostly, run smoke-first during the test phase as you said. One caveat to note:
  DataProtection.EntityFrameworkCore persists key-ring rows, so a benchmark run can write a key row to the shared DB — it'll reuse an existing valid key in practice, but it's not strictly zero-write, and
  you should know that before I run it 10 times.

  ---
  Which tooling option do you want for the Rider side — install the JetBrains MCP, re-point roslyn-lens at Consumer, or proceed with dotnet build plus project-rag? Once that's settled I'll write the spec
  and cut the branch.

      112  DwarfMapper ships an ambient facade, `IDwarfMapper`, with `Map<TDest>(object source)` — near-verbatim
      113  compatible with AutoMapper's `IMapper`. So:
      114
      115 -- Injected `IMapper` → `IDwarfMapper`. **All 693 call sites stay as written.**
      115 +- Injected `IMapper` → `IDwarfMapper`. **682 of the 693 call sites stay as written.**
      116  - The 7 `services.AddAutoMapper(typeof(XProfile))` → `services.AddDwarfMappers().ValidateDwarfMaps()`.
      117  - `Consumer.Web` (the composition root that references every provider) gets
      118    `[assembly: DwarfMapperValidationRoot]`, so a pair with no provider is a **`DWARF061` build error** rather
      119    than a runtime `DwarfMapMissingException`.
      120
      121 +#### The 11 call sites that do *not* survive
      122 +
      123 +`IDwarfMapper` declares only `Map<TDest>(object)` and `Map<TSource,TDest>(TSource)`. **Both construct a new
      124 +destination — there is no update-into overload on the facade.** Verified by reading `IDwarfMapper.cs`, not
      125 +inferred from the migration guide.
      126 +
      127 +| Call shape | N | Where | Resolution |
      128 +|---|---|---|---|
      129 +| `Map(src, existingDest)` | 1 | `CommandsManager` | Inject the **concrete** mapper; use its update-into `Map(S, T)` partial method |
      130 +| `Map(src, existingDest, typeof(S), typeof(D))` | 9 | `GiveawaysManager`, `StoreManager`, `CommandsService` ×3, `GiveawaysService`, `StoreService` ×2, `LiveStreamStatisticsService` | Sa
          +me, **plus** the runtime `Map(obj, srcType, destType)` form is an explicit non-goal — the types are statically known at every one of these sites, so they become direct typed calls |
      131 +| `Map<D>(src, opts => opts.Items.Add(...))` | 1 | `LiveStreamStatisticsService:50` | No `ResolutionContext`. Becomes an extra typed method parameter: `partial DbLiveStreamStatistics Map
          +(LiveStreamStatistics s, string userId)` |
      132 +
      133 +These sites are **why** the self-maps (`BotSettings→BotSettings`, `RankDto→RankDto`) and the 9
      134 +`SkipNullSourceMembers` patch-merge maps exist: they serve AutoMapper's merge overload. Update-into is
      135 +supported by DwarfMapper — via the concrete mapper, reading **method-level** `[MapProperty]`/`[MapIgnore]` —
      136 +just not through the ambient facade. `[MapCollectionKey]` (update-into only) is available where a `List<T>`
      137 +member should merge by key rather than be replaced.
      138 +
      139 +**Consequence:** the projects containing these 11 sites must inject the concrete mapper class alongside (or
      140 +instead of) `IDwarfMapper`. This is the one place the migration is not a near-verbatim swap.
      141 +
      142  ### 5.2 Profiles become attribute-decorated partial classes
      143
      144  One `[DwarfMapper]` partial class per existing profile, in a `Mapping/` folder replacing `AutoMapper/`.
  ⎿  Allowed by auto mode classifier

      153  | `.ForMember(d => d.X, o => o.MapFrom(_ => k))` | `[MapValue<B>(nameof(B.X), k)]` |
      154  | `.ConstructUsing(f)` | `[MapConstructor<A,B>(nameof(Factory))]` |
      155
      156 -A named partial method is only needed where a pair wants `[BeforeMap]`/`[AfterMap]`/`[ReverseMap]`/`[RoundTrip]`
      157 -hooks. This is a much smaller diff than a method-per-pair translation.
      156 +A named partial method is needed in three cases: hooks
      157 +(`[BeforeMap]`/`[AfterMap]`/`[ReverseMap]`/`[RoundTrip]`), update-into (§5.1), and **same-source-multi-target
      158 +pairs**.
      159
      160 +#### Same-source-multi-target → DWARF060
      161 +
      162 +Two `[GenerateMap]` attributes sharing a source type on one class collide (`DWARF060`, formerly a raw
      163 +`CS0111`), because the generated methods would have identical signatures. Each such pair needs a **named
      164 +partial method** instead. Measured:
      165 +
      166 +| Profile | Source type | Targets |
      167 +|---|---|---|
      168 +| `DtosMappingProfile` | `CommandOverviewDataDto` | 4 |
      169 +| | `GiveawayData`, `DiscordNotificationRequest`, `TimedMessage`, `GiveawayOverviewDataDto` | 2 each |
      170 +| `RepositoryMappingProfile` | `UserLexicon`, `BotSettings` | 2 each |
      171 +| `ServicesMappingProfile` | `UserCommand` | 3 |
      172 +| | `AliasCommand`, `StoreItem` | 2 each |
      173 +
      174 +**10 source types, 21 pairs.** So §5.2's attribute-only translation covers the large majority, but not all —
      175 +these 21 get named methods with method-level attributes.
      176 +
      177  **Lambdas become named methods.** `MapFrom(s => Compute(s.Y))` → a private `Compute` referenced by `Use=`;
      178  `Condition(s => p)` → a `bool` method referenced by `When=`.

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\docs\superpowers\specs\2026-08-11-automapper-to-dwarfmapper-design.md)
  ⎿  Added 22 lines, removed 6 lines
      236
      237  ### Leg 1: parity snapshots
      238
      239 -New `tests/Consumer.Mapping.ParityTests`. For each of the ~300 directed pairs:
      239 +New `tests/Consumer.Mapping.ParityTests`.
      240
      241 -1. `DwarfMapper.Testing.Fuzzer.Generate<TSource>(n, seed)` — deterministic, seeded fixtures (never hand-built
      242 -   uniform literals).
      243 -2. Map, and snapshot the result.
      241 +**Where the 300 typed invocations come from.** `Fuzzer.Generate<TSource>` is generic, so 300 pairs cannot be
      242 +enumerated at runtime from a list of `Type`s. A one-time generation script reflects over the live
      243 +`MapperConfiguration.GetAllTypeMaps()` — AutoMapper's own public API, which makes the inventory *authoritative*
      244 +rather than grep-derived — and emits two artifacts:
      245
      245 -Snapshots are generated and committed **while still on AutoMapper**, then re-asserted against DwarfMapper using
      246 -`GraphOracleComparer.CrossTypeDiff`. This is what lets AutoMapper's behaviour outlive AutoMapper's deletion.
      246 +- `mapping-manifest.json` — every directed pair with its source/target types and configured member rules.
      247 +- Generated parity-test source — one typed test per pair.
      248
      249 +The manifest is committed and becomes the checklist driving conversion, the parity tests, and the DWARF001
      250 +ledger. It is generated **once**, on AutoMapper, before any conversion.
      251 +
      252 +**Fixture reproducibility.** Snapshots are generated and committed while still on AutoMapper, then re-asserted
      253 +against DwarfMapper using `GraphOracleComparer.CrossTypeDiff`.
      254 +
      255 +The **source fixtures are serialized and committed alongside the output snapshots** — not merely the seed. The
      256 +seed alone would be sufficient only if `DwarfMapper.Testing.Fuzzer` were bit-identical across both runs, and it
      257 +will not be guaranteed to be: this migration references DwarfMapper by `ProjectReference` against a **live
      258 +repo** precisely so generator fixes can land mid-flight. A Fuzzer change between the "before" and "after" runs
      259 +would silently reshuffle inputs and invalidate every comparison while still appearing to pass.
      260 +
      261 +Serialized inputs make the comparison independent of the fixture generator. The DwarfMapper commit hash is also
      262 +recorded in the results for traceability.
      263 +
      264  **Divergences require an explicit, documented allowlist entry** — never a silently refreshed snapshot. An
      265  intentional divergence (e.g. a member AutoMapper was dropping that DwarfMapper now maps) is a design decision
      266  worth a line of justification.
  ⎿  Allowed by auto mode classifier

● Done for this phase. Here's where things stand.

  What's in place

  Branch: mapper-to-dwarf-transition, cut off develop in ConsumerSolution. Clean.

  Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-automapper-to-dwarfmapper-design.md. Not committed — .gitignore:277 excludes Docs/superpowers/* and no superpowers doc in that repo is tracked, so I followed your
  existing convention rather than forcing it in.

  Tasks: 8, sequenced so the branch compiles at every commit and AutoMapper comes out last.

  Three things verification caught that the design had wrong

  Studying the options surface and checking claims empirically changed real scope:

  1. NullStrategy defaults to Throw. A nullable-value source mapping to a non-nullable target throws where AutoMapper silently wrote default. Would have built clean and failed in production. Parity needs
  NullStrategy = SetDefault alongside EnumStrategy = ByValue.

  2. "All 693 call sites stay verbatim" was wrong — it's 682. I read IDwarfMapper.cs rather than trusting the migration guide: both its methods construct a new destination, and there is no update-into
  overload on the facade. Eleven sites need real work:
  - 10 update-into Map(src, dest, ...) calls — and 9 of those use the 4-arg runtime-type form, which is doubly unsupported. They must inject the concrete mapper.
  - LiveStreamStatisticsService:50 uses ResolutionContext.Items, which becomes an extra typed parameter.

  Those sites are precisely why the self-maps and the 9 SkipNullSourceMembers maps exist — they serve AutoMapper's merge overload.

  3. 21 pairs across 3 profiles hit DWARF060 (same source type, multiple targets — CommandOverviewDataDto alone has 4). Those need named partial methods, not attributes.

  The offsetting good news: class-level pair-scoped attributes mean the other ~280 pairs translate as attributes with no method at all, and [MapConstructor<S,T>] handles the 9 ConstructUsing cases
  directly.

  Two things I need from you

  1. Open Consumer.sln in Rider — the MCP is connected but has DwarfMapper.NET loaded, so IDE tools can't reach ConsumerSolution yet.
  2. Review the spec. The parts most worth your eye are §5.1 (the 11 call sites — this is the only place the migration isn't a near-verbatim swap) and §6 (the three parity settings).

  Once you've read it I'll turn it into an implementation plan and start on task #1.

  One thing I deliberately did not do: fabricate any benchmark numbers. Level B smoke-runs during the test phase as you asked, and if the hosts can't boot without your real secrets, I'll say so and
  degrade to the in-process measurement rather than invent figures.

  Three things worth knowing:

  .targets, not .props. Directory.Build.props imports before the csproj body, so a UseDwarfMapper property set in a project wouldn't exist yet when the condition evaluated. .targets imports after. This
  also meant I didn't have to touch your existing props file.

  Rider's buildIsSuccess is unreliable here — it reported false with no diagnostics on a solution the CLI builds with exit 0. I've noted in the spec that dotnet build is the gate; Rider stays useful for
  problems/inspections/refactorings. Its Problems view is where the AutoMapper advisory surfaced.

  Wiring is proven in 1 of 7 projects. The other 6 compile, but with no [DwarfMapper] classes yet that only proves the reference resolves, not that the analyzer runs there. The failure mode is loud and
  documented (CS8795), and each project's first conversion doubles as its wiring check — I'll put that trap explicitly in the parallel agents' instructions so nobody burns an hour debugging attribute
  syntax when the real cause is a missing opt-in.

  On your parallelization suggestion: agreed for tasks 4 and 5 — 12 profiles is a clean fan-out with no shared state. It needs task 3's snapshots in place first, otherwise parallel agents would be
  rewriting ~300 maps with only 16 mapping tests to catch a regression.

  Moving on to task 2, the benchmark harness. Following your perf-harness/ convention with a sibling console project, and giving it its own UseDwarfMapper opt-in from the start even though the DwarfMapper
  side stays dormant until task 8 — so the same binary measures both sides.

● Confirmed exactly. UserId holds a private readonly int with no public properties — and carries [JsonConverter(typeof(UserIdJsonConverter))] from Newtonsoft, which System.Text.Json ignores entirely. So
  every strong-typed ID serialized to {} on both sides: the comparison is empty-vs-empty, and vacuous comparisons pass. UserId appears in most DTOs.

  Switching the snapshot serializer to Newtonsoft — which the API itself uses, and which honours those converters.

      109      private static string Mb(long bytes) =>
      110          string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, $"{bytes / 1024.0 / 1024.0:F2} MB");
      111
      112 +    /// <summary>
      113 +    ///     The DwarfMapper commit these numbers were produced against. Necessary because DwarfMapper is
      114 +    ///     consumed by <c>ProjectReference</c> against a live sibling repo, so "DwarfMapper" is a moving
      115 +    ///     target — a before/after comparison is only meaningful alongside the revision it ran on.
      116 +    ///     Read straight from the git plumbing to avoid shelling out.
      117 +    /// </summary>
      118 +    internal static string DwarfMapperRevision()
      119 +    {
      120 +        try
      121 +        {
      122 +            var repo = Path.Combine(Directory.GetParent(RepoRoot())!.FullName, "DwarfMapper.NET");
      123 +            var gitDir = Path.Combine(repo, ".git");
      124 +            if (!Directory.Exists(gitDir))
      125 +            {
      126 +                return "unknown (not a git checkout)";
      127 +            }
      128 +
      129 +            var head = File.ReadAllText(Path.Combine(gitDir, "HEAD")).Trim();
      130 +            if (!head.StartsWith("ref:", StringComparison.Ordinal))
      131 +            {
      132 +                return head; // detached HEAD already holds the sha
      133 +            }
      134 +
      135 +            var refPath = Path.Combine(gitDir, head[4..].Trim().Replace('/', Path.DirectorySeparatorChar));
      136 +            if (File.Exists(refPath))
      137 +            {
      138 +                return File.ReadAllText(refPath).Trim();
      139 +            }
      140 +
      141 +            // Packed refs: the loose ref file does not exist.
      142 +            var packed = Path.Combine(gitDir, "packed-refs");
      143 +            if (File.Exists(packed))
      144 +            {
      145 +                var name = head[4..].Trim();
      146 +                foreach (var line in File.ReadAllLines(packed))
      147 +                {
      148 +                    if (line.EndsWith(" " + name, StringComparison.Ordinal))
      149 +                    {
      150 +                        return line.Split(' ')[0];
      151 +                    }
      152 +                }
      153 +            }
      154 +
      155 +            return "unknown (ref not resolvable)";
      156 +        }
      157 +        catch (IOException)
      158 +        {
      159 +            return "unknown (io error)";
      160 +        }
      161 +    }
      162 +
      163 +    private static string RepoRoot()
      164 +    {
      165 +        var dir = AppContext.BaseDirectory;
      166 +        while (dir is not null && !File.Exists(Path.Combine(dir, "Consumer.sln")))
      167 +        {
      168 +            dir = Path.GetDirectoryName(dir.TrimEnd(Path.DirectorySeparatorChar));
      169 +        }
      170 +
      171 +        return dir ?? Directory.GetCurrentDirectory();
      172 +    }
      173 +

      175      {
      176          // Walk up from the binary to the repo root, which is the directory holding Consumer.sln.
  ⎿  Allowed by auto mode classifier

  Task 3 — golden master ✅ But it took two corrections to become trustworthy:

  - Strong-typed IDs serialized as {}. UserId wraps a private field and carries a Newtonsoft converter that System.Text.Json ignores. Both sides captured empty, so every strong-typed member compared
  empty-vs-empty — and vacuous comparisons pass. Switched to Newtonsoft; now "UserId": 1038734969.
  - 24 snapshots couldn't be replayed. Verifying the master against AutoMapper itself — a pure self-test that must be 100% — gave 254/278. Ten types can't round-trip JSON; fourteen produce different
  output from the same mapper (lossy round-trip on get-only members). Sealed as unverifiable rather than counted as coverage.

  Net: 254 provably replayable pairs (re-verify 254/254, exit 0), plus 44 explicitly flagged for manual review.

  On Level B — the start/stop benchmark you asked for. The real API host isn't measurable here: BotLoaderBackgroundService pages 18,450 Primary bots over HTTP before the server listens, then bot validation
  fails on empty OAuth credentials — no /health in 420 s. Rather than fabricate numbers, it measures the full service graph instead (307 registrations, 294 resolved, 10/10 runs): working set 113.9 MB,
  CPU 1765 ms, provider build 258 ms, with real Win32 I/O counters. That's the honest measurable subset of "load all the services" — and it's stated as such in the report.

● Two agents down, seven running. Two findings from them worth surfacing now.

  Confirmed — a silent data corruption this migration would have caused. DispatchChannel.NextDay carries [Description("Next-Day")]. DwarfMapper's built-in enum→string prefers [Description] over the identifier,
  so it would persist "Next-Day" into MongoDB where AutoMapper's .ToString() has always written "NextDay" — breaking reads of every existing document. The agent pinned it with an explicit converter. I verified
  the enum myself; this one is real.

  Reported but not yet verified — a possible DwarfMapper generator defect: a class-level [MapProperty<A,B>(..., Use = ...)] converter appearing to also apply to a different target member that auto-matches
  the same source member, with no diagnostic and a green build. If real it affects every agent's work, so I tried to reproduce it in isolation — but the build failed on obj/ corruption from the seven
  concurrent agent builds, which is the shared-state cost of the fan-out. I've removed my repro file (it lived in Tertiary, which Omega depends on, so it risked poisoning their builds) and will verify
  properly once they're done. I'm not treating that claim as established until I've seen it myself.

  Also of note: the ChatHistory agent deliberately deviated from my instruction — I said translate all three expressions as Use= methods, and it argued SenderBadges should instead be left unconfigured,
  because DwarfMapper's default AsEmpty collection strategy already is the ?? new List<>() coalesce, and a Use= method would have to hand-roll element mapping the declared pair already generates. It
  quoted the generated code as evidence. That's the right call and better than what I asked for.

  I'll consolidate all the DWARF001 decisions into the ledger once the remaining seven land, then verify the whole branch builds together and run the parity suite against DwarfMapper.

      66  - `.ValidateMemberList(MemberList.Destination)` — **dropped, no equivalent needed** — DwarfMapper's default `RequiredMapping = Target` is the same check, enforced on every build rather th
          an only under `AssertConfigurationIsValid()`.
      67  - **Self-maps** (`RankDto→RankDto`, `StoreItemDataDto→StoreItemDataDto`, `TimedMessageDto→TimedMessageDto`, `GiveawayChatterDto→GiveawayChatterDto`) translated as **clone** pairs. They or
          iginally served AutoMapper's two-argument `Map(source, existingDestination)` **merge** overload — the call sites that need merge semantics are handled in the DI/call-site step, not here.
      68
      69 +### Consumer.Omega — `AdminMappingProfile` → `AdminMappers`
      70 +**No decisions required** — all eight model/DTO pairs are structural mirror images and every destination
      71 +member name-matches a compatible source member.
      72 +- The profile's single `ForMember` (`ReferredByUserId` mapped through `s.ReferredByUserId != null ? … : null`)
      73 +  is **semantically dead** and deliberately not carried over. Both sides are `UserId?`, and under `UserId`'s
      74 +  overloaded `operator !=` the ternary reduces to the identity. Plain name-matching emits the same assignment.
      75 +- `FeatureTopUser.UserId → FeatureTopUserDto.UserId` needs no configuration: DwarfMapper picks up the
      76 +  `implicit operator int(UserId)` on the strong type — the same operator AutoMapper used.
      77 +
      78 +### Consumer.Omega — `ChattersMappingProfile` → `ChattersMappers`
      79 +Ten `ChatterData` members **ignored**, all inherited from the profile's own `.Ignore()` list: a bulk-import
      80 +DTO (`ImportChatterDataDto`) carries no data for them, so each keeps what the `ChatterData` constructor set.
      81 +`DisplayName`, `IsBroadcaster`, `IsModerator`, `IsSubscriber`, `ProfileImageUrl`, `ChatterRank`,
      82 +`MessagesSent`, `Badges`, `IsBot`, `IsBlacklisted`.
      83 +- `ChatterDataDto.ChatterId` — **mapped** — rename from `ChatterData.Id`.
      84 +- `Rank` reverse direction — **mapped via `CreateRank`, which names the 4-argument constructor explicitly.**
      85 +  Load-bearing: the 3-argument overload mints a fresh `Guid` and would silently lose `Uid`.
      86 +- `ChattersPageRequest.OrderBy` — **mapped** with `When = HasOrderBy`, the profile's `PreCondition`.
      87 +
      88 +**Carried-over latent bug — deliberately not fixed.** `ChatterData.ChatterRank` is `Rank?` but
      89 +`ChatterDataDto.ChatterRank` is `required RankDto` (non-nullable). The generated helper emits
      90 +`if (s is null) return null!;`, putting a null into a member declared non-nullable. **This is exactly what
      91 +AutoMapper does today**, so it was preserved rather than silently changed — the migration's job is parity.
      92 +Substituting `RankDto.Empty` would alter behaviour and mint a random `Guid` on every map. Worth a separate
      93 +decision, outside this migration.
      94 +

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\Docs\mapping-migration-ledger.md)
  ⎿  Added 30 lines
       92  Substituting `RankDto.Empty` would alter behaviour and mint a random `Guid` on every map. Worth a separate
       93  decision, outside this migration.
       94
       95 +### Consumer.Repositories — `UserMappingProfile` → `UserMappers`
       96 +No `DWARF001` fired; every destination member resolved by name. Three items need recording.
       97 +
       98 +- `StreamerProfileDataDocument.Id` — **`[MapValue("")]`**. The profile did `.Ignore()`, but `Id` is
       99 +  `required`, so `[MapIgnore]` yields an illegal object initializer (`CS9035`). AutoMapper's expression trees
      100 +  bypass that compile-time rule and left `Id` **null**. `""` is the closest legal stand-in and is never
      101 +  observable: `StreamerProfileRepository.CreateOrUpdateAsync` is the only producer and overwrites
      102 +  `document.Id` on the next line.
      103 +
      104 +- `ButtonSlots` (document → domain) — **hand-written `Use = ToButtonSlots`.** The profile's
      105 +  `PreCondition(src => src.ButtonSlots is not null)` is **load-bearing, not cosmetic**: `ButtonsCard.razor`
      106 +  indexes `ButtonSlots[row*2]` and `[row*2+1]` up to slot 7, so a null document array must produce the domain
      107 +  type's `new ProfileButtonData?[8]`. Neither `When=` (a `required` member cannot be left unassigned) nor the
      108 +  default `NullCollectionStrategy.AsEmpty` (length 0 → `IndexOutOfRangeException`) reproduces that.
      109 +
      110 +- **Known divergence: `string → BotPlatform` is now case-sensitive.** The generated switch matches exact
      111 +  identifiers; AutoMapper's `Enum.Parse` ignored case, and unrecognized names now throw
      112 +  `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` rather than `ArgumentException`. Only reachable via hand-edited Mongo data,
      113 +  since the writer emits the exact identifier.
      114 +
      115 +**Five `DWARF070` warnings left standing on purpose.** `ProfileBannerText`, `Description`, `SubDescription`,
      116 +`OfflineMessageTitle`, `OfflineMessage` are `string?` on the domain model and non-nullable `string` on the
      117 +document. AutoMapper wrote the null straight through. `NullSubstitute = ""` would change **what is persisted
      118 +to MongoDB** — that is a data decision, not a migration one, so the warning stays until someone makes it
      119 +deliberately.
      120 +
      121 +*(`BotPlatform ↔ string` needed no converter: the enum carries no `[EnumMember]`/`[Description]`, so
      122 +DwarfMapper emits the identifier form — byte-identical to AutoMapper's `ToString()`. Contrast
      123 +`DispatchChannel`, behaviour change 1 above, where exactly that attribute changed the output.)*
      124 +

● Update(tests\DwarfMapper.Generator.Tests\SelfMapDiagnosticTests.cs)
  ⎿  Added 53 lines
      138
      139          Assert.Contains("Dto", generated, StringComparison.Ordinal);
      140      }
      141 +
      142 +    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      143 +    // Regression: found migrating Consumer (~300 maps) off AutoMapper 14.
      144 +    //
      145 +    // That codebase has four legitimate CreateMap<X, X>() clone maps, so it hits DWARF076 four times by
      146 +    // design. docs/diagnostics.md#dwarf076 offers three escape hatches:
      147 +    //
      148 +    //     "(#pragma warning disable DWARF076, a [SuppressMessage], or
      149 +    //      dotnet_diagnostic.DWARF076.severity = none in .editorconfig)"
      150 +    //
      151 +    // Only the third was ever covered by a test — and the test above does not actually assert
      152 +    // suppression, it only asserts the code still compiles. The two in-FILE hatches are what a consumer
      153 +    // reaches for first, because they are local to the deliberate clone instead of disabling the rule
      154 +    // for the whole project. If they do not work, a warnings-as-errors consumer has no local way out
      155 +    // and the documentation is actively misleading.
      156 +    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      157 +
      158 +    [Fact]
      159 +    public void DWARF076_is_suppressed_by_a_pragma_at_the_declaration_site()
      160 +    {
      161 +        const string src = """
      162 +            using DwarfMapper;
      163 +            namespace Demo;
      164 +            public sealed class Dto { public int Id { get; set; } }
      165 +
      166 +            #pragma warning disable DWARF076
      167 +            [DwarfMapper]

● Update(tests\DwarfMapper.Generator.Tests\MapPropertyTests.cs)
  ⎿  Added 78 lines
      176          // read-only target is not in writableByName -> treated as unknown/un-writable target
      177          Assert.Contains(diagnostics, d => d.Id == "DWARF008");
      178      }
      179 +
      180 +    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      181 +    // Regression: found migrating Consumer (~300 maps) off AutoMapper 14.
      182 +    //
      183 +    // A pair-scoped [MapProperty<S,T>(src, tgt, Use = M)] names ONE destination member. If the same
      184 +    // SOURCE member also auto-matches a DIFFERENT destination member by name, the converter must not
      185 +    // reach that second member — the attribute did not name it.
      186 +    //
      187 +    // Why this matters more than it looks: the failure is silent. The build stays green, no diagnostic
      188 +    // fires, and the only symptom is wrong data. In the case that surfaced it, DonationDetails.DonationId
      189 +    // fed both PremiumDocument.Id (via a Use= that prefixes a date, "20260811_<guid>") and
      190 +    // PremiumDocument.DonationId (a plain auto-matched copy). Leaking the converter would have written
      191 +    // the decorated document id into the plain donation-id column of every new premium record.
      192 +    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      193 +
      194 +    [Fact]
      195 +    public void Pair_scoped_Use_converter_applies_only_to_the_member_it_names()
      196 +    {
      197 +        const string src = """
      198 +                           using System;
      199 +                           using DwarfMapper;
      200 +                           namespace Demo;
      201 +                           public class Source { public Guid Code { get; set; } }
      202 +                           public class Target
      203 +                           {
      204 +                               public string Tag { get; set; } = "";
      205 +                               public string Code { get; set; } = "";
      206 +                           }
      207 +
      208 +                           [DwarfMapper]
      209 +                           [GenerateMap<Source, Target>]
      210 +                           [MapProperty<Source, Target>(nameof(Source.Code), nameof(Target.Tag), Use = nameof(Decorate))]
      211 +                           public partial class M
      212 +                           {
      213 +                               private static string Decorate(Guid g) => "X_" + g;
      214 +                           }
      215 +                           """;
      216 +
      217 +        var generated = GeneratorAssert.EmitsCompilableCode(src);
      218 +
      219 +        // The named member gets the converter...
      220 +        Assert.Contains("Tag = Decorate(", generated, StringComparison.Ordinal);
      221 +
      222 +        // ...and the member that merely shares the SOURCE does not.
      223 +        Assert.DoesNotContain("Code = Decorate(", generated, StringComparison.Ordinal);
      224 +    }
      225 +
      226 +    [Fact]
      227 +    public void Pair_scoped_Use_converter_does_not_leak_to_a_second_target_of_the_same_source()
      228 +    {
      229 +        // Same rule across pairs rather than within one: configuring S -> A must not colour S -> B.
      230 +        const string src = """
      231 +                           using System;
      232 +                           using DwarfMapper;
      233 +                           namespace Demo;
      234 +                           public class Source { public Guid Code { get; set; } }
      235 +                           public class TargetA { public string Code { get; set; } = ""; }
      236 +                           public class TargetB { public string Code { get; set; } = ""; }
      237 +
      238 +                           [DwarfMapper]
      239 +                           [GenerateMap<Source, TargetA>]
      240 +                           [GenerateMap<Source, TargetB>]
      241 +                           [MapProperty<Source, TargetA>(nameof(Source.Code), nameof(TargetA.Code), Use = nameof(Decorate))]
      242 +                           public partial class M
      243 +                           {
      244 +                               private static string Decorate(Guid g) => "X_" + g;
      245 +                           }
      246 +                           """;
      247 +
      248 +        var generated = GeneratorAssert.EmitsCompilableCode(src);
      249 +
      250 +        // Exactly one call site: the TargetA pair. TargetB must use the plain conversion.
      251 +        var occurrences = generated.Split("Decorate(", StringSplitOptions.None).Length - 1;
      252 +
      253 +        // One in the helper's own declaration is not counted because the declaration reads
      254 +        // "Decorate(global::System.Guid g)" only in user code, not the generated file.
      255 +        Assert.Equal(1, occurrences);
      256 +    }
      257  }

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\Docs\mapping-migration-ledger.md)
  ⎿  Added 32 lines
      122  DwarfMapper emits the identifier form — byte-identical to AutoMapper's `ToString()`. Contrast
      123  `DispatchChannel`, behaviour change 1 above, where exactly that attribute changed the output.)*
      124
      125 +### Consumer.Omega — `RepositoryMappingProfile` → `RepositoryMappers` + `RepositoryLexiconPatchMappers`
      126 +84 directions (54 `CreateMap` + 30 `ReverseMap`), split across **two classes** because
      127 +`SkipNullSourceMembers` is a class-level option while AutoMapper's `ForAllMembers` was per-map.
      128 +
      129 +- **Four `…Document.Id` members** — **`Use = UnassignedDocumentId` (`null!`)**. The profile ignored them, but
      130 +  each is `required`, so `[MapIgnore]` is `CS9035`. AutoMapper built the record reflectively and left them
      131 +  null; the repository assigns the real id on the next line in every case (verified per call site).
      132 +- **`AlertsSettingsDocument` / `AlertsLexiconDocument` platform members** — **mapped** from the single
      133 +  `ClientAlerts` dictionary they derive from. `[MapIgnore]` + `[AfterMap]` was impossible: all ten are
      134 +  `required`, so omitting them from the initializer failed with ten `CS9035`s.
      135 +- **`AlertsSettings.ClientAlerts` / `AlertsLexicon.ClientAlerts`** — **ignored + `[AfterMap]`**. These are
      136 +  built from the *whole* source, which `Use=` forbids (`DWARF014`).
      137 +- **`Rank` construction** — `[MapConstructor]` naming the explicit factory. `Rank` has two constructors and
      138 +  the 3-argument one invents a fresh `Uid`; letting the generator choose would have been a silent data bug.
      139 +- **Collection↔object maps** (`ICollection<Rank> ↔ UserRanksDocument`, quotes twin) — hand-written methods
      140 +  delegating to the generated element pairs. Not a DwarfMapper mapping shape.
      141 +
      142 +**Known parity gap — `ForAllMembers` does not reach nested maps.** The four patch-class pairs correctly emit
      143 +`if (src.X is not null)` guards, but `UserLexiconDocument → UserLexicon` lives in the main class and
      144 +**synthesizes its own private copies** of the nested document→model helpers, which carry **no null guards**.
      145 +AutoMapper reused the registered, guarded maps. The pair cannot move into the patch class because it also
      146 +nests `AlertsLexiconDocument → AlertsLexicon`, which carries an `[AfterMap]` hook. **This is a real
      147 +behavioural difference, not a cosmetic one** — Mongo can deserialize nulls into these members.
      148 +
      149 +**Self-maps are shallow here** (`CloneBotSettings`, `CloneUserLexicon`): they copy nested members by
      150 +reference where AutoMapper deep-mapped. This follows the `ServerMappers` precedent, and also sidesteps an
      151 +otherwise fatal problem — a deep clone would have to construct the abstract `ClientAlertSettings` /
      152 +`ClientAlertLexicon` dictionary values.
      153 +
      154 +*(Nine more `DWARF070` left standing for the same reason as `UserMappers`: every suggested fix would write a
      155 +different value to MongoDB than AutoMapper's null.)*
      156 +
      157  ---

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\Docs\mapping-migration-ledger.md)
  ⎿  Added 31 lines
      154  *(Nine more `DWARF070` left standing for the same reason as `UserMappers`: every suggested fix would write a
      155  different value to MongoDB than AutoMapper's null.)*
      156
      157 +### Consumer.Services — `ServicesMappingProfile` → `ServicesMappers`
      158 +24 map methods; five `IncludeBase` sites restated (one more than the brief predicted), each bracketed by
      159 +`MIRRORS BASE` / `END MIRRORS BASE` comments naming the base pair and its deviations.
      160 +
      161 +- `DbGiveaway.JoinedUsers` — **`[MapValue("")]`** — profile ignored it, but it is `required` (`CS9035`).
      162 +  `string.Empty` is what `GiveawaysService` writes by hand, and it overwrites this one line after mapping.
      163 +- `UserCommand.ForbiddenPlatforms` — **`Use = ToEmptyPlatformSet`** rather than `[MapIgnore<TTarget>]`.
      164 +  `[MapIgnore<T>]` is *target*-scoped and would also have silenced the member on the
      165 +  `UserCommand → UserCommand` self-copy, which the profile **does** copy.
      166 +- `ResolutionContext.Items` → an ordinary method parameter: `ToDbStatistics(LiveStreamStatistics, int ownerId)`.
      167 +
      168 +**BLOCKER — `DbCommand → CustomizedCommand` is omitted.** `CustomizedCommand`'s only constructor is
      169 +**private**, so the generator reports `DWARF026` and there is no escape: `private` is excluded from
      170 +`AllowNonPublic` by design, and a factory cannot reach past a private constructor. **AutoMapper built this
      171 +type by reflection.** `CommandsService.cs:99` consumes the pair, so this must be resolved before AutoMapper
      172 +is removed. Fix is a source change in `Consumer.Core.DataStructures`: make the constructor `internal` and add
      173 +`[InternalsVisibleTo]`, then `AllowNonPublic = true`. That is the deliberate, compile-time-checked version of
      174 +what AutoMapper was doing unsafely.
      175 +
      176 +**Behavioural divergence — `Identifier` is lost on the two `UserCommand`-constructing maps.** Under
      177 +`[MapConstructor]` the factory owns construction, so `init`-only members cannot be assigned afterwards
      178 +(`CS8852`), and `UserCommand.Empty` mints a fresh `Guid`. A `UserCommand` mapped from a row no longer carries
      179 +that row's `Identifier`. Same source-side fix as the blocker above.
      180 +
      181 +**The three `→ Command` maps are update-into**, because `Command` is abstract and cannot be constructed. They
      182 +cannot overwrite `init`-only members on the existing instance, where AutoMapper's reflection did.
      183 +
      184 +*(Cutover note: `StoreService`, `GiveawaysService` and `CommandsService` consume several of these pairs
      185 +**update-into**. Only create-maps are declared here, matching the profile's literal shape;
      186 +`partial void Update(…)` companions are needed when those call sites switch over.)*
      187 +

      169 -**private**, so the generator reports `DWARF026` and there is no escape: `private` is excluded from
      170 -`AllowNonPublic` by design, and a factory cannot reach past a private constructor. **AutoMapper built this
      171 -type by reflection.** `CommandsService.cs:99` consumes the pair, so this must be resolved before AutoMapper
      172 -is removed. Fix is a source change in `Consumer.Core.DataStructures`: make the constructor `internal` and add
      173 -`[InternalsVisibleTo]`, then `AllowNonPublic = true`. That is the deliberate, compile-time-checked version of
      174 -what AutoMapper was doing unsafely.
      168 +**RESOLVED — `DbCommand → CustomizedCommand` was omitted, and is now restored.**
      169 +`CustomizedCommand`'s only constructor was `private`, carrying the comment *"Empty ctor for automapper"* —
      170 +which worked **only** because AutoMapper constructs by reflection, ignoring accessibility at runtime.
      171 +DwarfMapper emits ordinary C#, so the map failed with `DWARF026`, and `CommandsService.cs:99` consumes it.
      172
      173 +The fix, on the maintainer's instruction, is the deliberate version of the same grant:
      174 +
      175 +1. Constructor widened `private` → **`internal`** in `Consumer.Core.DataStructures`.
      176 +2. `[InternalsVisibleTo]` for `Consumer.Services` and `Consumer.Omega`, following the repo's existing
      177 +   `AssemblyAttribute` convention.
      178 +3. `[DwarfMapper(AllowNonPublic = true)]` on `ServicesMappers`.
      179 +4. Declared as a **named partial method** (`ToCustomizedCommand`), because `DbCommand` already targets
      180 +   `UserCommand` on that class and two same-source pairs collide (`DWARF060`).
      181 +
      182 +**This is strictly better than the AutoMapper behaviour it replaces**, and not only because the grant is now
      183 +compiler-checked. Verified in the emitted code:
      184 +
      185 +```csharp
      186 +CustomizedFor = source.CustomizedFor!,
      187 +Identifier    = source.Identifier,      // init-only, and assigned
      188 +```
      189 +
      190 +Direct construction fills an **object initializer**, where `init`-only members are assignable — unlike the
      191 +`[MapConstructor]` factory path used for `UserCommand`, where the factory owns construction and `init`
      192 +members cannot be written afterwards (`CS8852`). **The same three-step fix would therefore also recover
      193 +`UserCommand.Identifier`** (divergence below); `UserCommand`'s parameterless constructor is still `private`.
      194 +`AllowNonPublic` cannot rescue it — `private` is never usable, by design, because the generated code could
      195 +not compile.

      214
      215  1. **`DWARF076` cannot be suppressed in-file.** `docs/diagnostics.md` says to use `#pragma warning disable DWARF076` or `[SuppressMessage]`. Neither works on a clean rebuild — Roslyn doe
           s not run generator-reported diagnostics through pragma filtering. Only `dotnet_diagnostic.DWARF076.severity = none` in `.editorconfig` does. A warnings-as-errors consumer has no in-file
            escape hatch. Either the doc or the suppression path needs fixing.
      216

      219 -   auto-matched the same **source** member, silently and with a green build. If real, a class-level `Use=`
      220 -   would be keyed on the source member rather than the (source, target) pair. **Not yet reproduced

      222 -   as established until confirmed.

● Root cause found — MapperExtractor.Conversions.cs:397-410. The generator scans all non-partial user methods for one whose signature matches srcType → tgtType and adopts it as an automatic converter.
  Decorate(Guid) → string matches Guid → string, so it's silently applied to Code.

  So it isn't Use= leaking — it's that a method written to serve one member via Use= gets adopted as a general-purpose converter. Let me confirm the explicit path is separate before fixing.

● Update(src\DwarfMapper.Generator\Pipeline\MapperExtractor.Conversions.cs)
  ⎿  Added 32 lines
      791          return false;
      792      }
      793
      794 +    /// <summary>
      795 +    ///     Every method name dedicated to a member by a <c>Use=</c> anywhere on this mapper — class-level
      796 +    ///     pair-scoped attributes and per-method ones alike.
      797 +    /// </summary>
      798 +    /// <remarks>
      799 +    ///     These are withheld from signature-based auto-adoption. Naming a converter for a member states
      800 +    ///     that it belongs to that member; it is not an offer to convert every pair of those types. The
      801 +    ///     scan is deliberately generic over the named argument rather than per-attribute-type, so it
      802 +    ///     covers <c>[MapProperty]</c>, <c>[MapValue]</c> and their generic pair-scoped forms uniformly,
      803 +    ///     and keeps working if another attribute gains a <c>Use=</c>.
      804 +    /// </remarks>
      805 +    private static HashSet<string> CollectReservedConverterNames(INamedTypeSymbol classSymbol)
      806 +    {
      807 +        var reserved = new HashSet<string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);
      808 +
      809 +        static void Scan(HashSet<string> into, System.Collections.Immutable.ImmutableArray<AttributeData> attrs)
      810 +        {
      811 +            foreach (var a in attrs)
      812 +                foreach (var na in a.NamedArguments)
      813 +                    if (string.Equals(na.Key, "Use", StringComparison.Ordinal)
      814 +                        && na.Value.Value is string s
      815 +                        && !string.IsNullOrEmpty(s))
      816 +                        into.Add(s);
      817 +        }
      818 +
      819 +        Scan(reserved, classSymbol.GetAttributes());
      820 +        foreach (var member in classSymbol.GetMembers())
      821 +            Scan(reserved, member.GetAttributes());
      822 +
      823 +        return reserved;
      824 +    }
      825 +

● I can honour [SuppressMessage] on the mapper class, which is the documented granularity. Adding a helper and wiring it in.

● Update(src\DwarfMapper.Generator\Pipeline\MapperExtractor.Conversions.cs)
  ⎿  Added 33 lines
      802      ///     covers <c>[MapProperty]</c>, <c>[MapValue]</c> and their generic pair-scoped forms uniformly,
      803      ///     and keeps working if another attribute gains a <c>Use=</c>.
      804      /// </remarks>
      805 +    /// <summary>
      806 +    ///     True when the symbol carries <c>[SuppressMessage(…, "DWARFxxx…")]</c> for the given id.
      807 +    /// </summary>
      808 +    /// <remarks>
      809 +    ///     Generator-reported diagnostics do not pass through Roslyn's pragma filtering, so
      810 +    ///     <c>#pragma warning disable</c> cannot reach them — that is a platform limitation, not something
      811 +    ///     this generator can fix. <c>[SuppressMessage]</c> however is an ordinary attribute the generator
      812 +    ///     can read, so honouring it restores an in-FILE escape hatch next to the deliberate code, instead
      813 +    ///     of forcing an <c>.editorconfig</c> entry that disables the rule for the whole project.
      814 +    ///     <para>
      815 +    ///         The checkId is matched by prefix because the conventional spelling carries a description
      816 +    ///         after a colon: <c>"DWARF076:Source and target are the same type"</c>.
      817 +    ///     </para>
      818 +    /// </remarks>
      819 +    private static bool HasSuppressMessage(ISymbol symbol, string diagnosticId)
      820 +    {
      821 +        foreach (var a in symbol.GetAttributes())
      822 +        {
      823 +            if (!string.Equals(a.AttributeClass?.Name, "SuppressMessageAttribute", StringComparison.Ordinal))
      824 +                continue;
      825 +
      826 +            if (a.ConstructorArguments.Length < 2)
      827 +                continue;
      828 +
      829 +            if (a.ConstructorArguments[1].Value is string checkId
      830 +                && (string.Equals(checkId, diagnosticId, StringComparison.Ordinal)
      831 +                    || checkId.StartsWith(diagnosticId + ":", StringComparison.Ordinal)))
      832 +                return true;
      833 +        }
      834 +
      835 +        return false;
      836 +    }
      837 +
      838      private static HashSet<string> CollectReservedConverterNames(INamedTypeSymbol classSymbol)
      839      {
      840          var reserved = new HashSet<string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);

      234 +would write `null` into a `required` non-nullable DTO member. A `Use=` converter must return the destination
      235 +type, so this throws `InvalidCastException` instead. Both call sites pre-split by `MessageType`, so it cannot
      236 +fire.
      237 +
      238 +**Known divergence — null-skip semantics.** AutoMapper's `srcMember != null` was a *runtime value* check;

● Done — regression tests written, both defects reproduced, and both fixed.

  The two DwarfMapper defects

  1. Use= converters were being auto-adopted elsewhere — confirmed, fixed.

  The agent's report was right, and the cause wasn't what either of us assumed. It isn't Use= "leaking": the generator scans all user methods for one whose signature matches srcType → tgtType and adopts
  it automatically. A string BuildDocumentId(Guid) written for Document.Id therefore also served Document.DonationId — silently, green build, no diagnostic. Every premium record would have stored
  "20260811_<guid>" in the plain donation-id field.

  Fix: a method named by any Use= is withheld from signature-based auto-adoption. Collected at mapper scope, not per-method — my first attempt reserved per-method, and the test caught that ToB still stole
  ToA's converter.

  2. DWARF076 had no working in-file escape hatch — partly fixed, partly a docs correction.

  Four agents independently hit this. diagnostics.md promised #pragma, [SuppressMessage], or .editorconfig; only the last worked.

  - [SuppressMessage] on the mapper is now honoured — it's an ordinary attribute the generator can read.
  - #pragma cannot be fixed here: pragmas are applied by compiler diagnostic filtering, which generator-reported diagnostics never pass through. That's a Roslyn limitation, so I corrected the docs rather
  than leaving a false promise, and pinned current behaviour in a test that will fail if Roslyn ever changes.

  Verification: 6,102 tests across all four suites, 0 failures. Committed to a branch (fix/use-converter-scoping-and-dwarf076-suppression) rather than master.

  Your internal instruction

  Done, and it paid off more than expected. CustomizedCommand's ctor was private with the comment "Empty ctor for automapper" — working only via reflection. Now internal + [InternalsVisibleTo], and the
  generated code confirms:

  CustomizedFor = source.CustomizedFor!,
  Identifier    = source.Identifier,      // init-only, and assigned

  Direct construction fills an object initializer where init members are assignable — unlike the [MapConstructor] factory path, which is exactly why UserCommand still loses Identifier. The same one-line
  fix would recover that too; its ctor is still private, and AllowNonPublic deliberately can't reach private.

  Migration status

  All 12 profiles converted. Full solution builds with 0 errors. Tasks 6–8 remain: the DI/call-site swap (including the 11 sites that don't survive verbatim), then removing AutoMapper and running the
  after-verification against the 254-pair golden master.

      247 +## Parity replay against DwarfMapper — first run
      248 +
      249 +`dotnet run --project bench-harness -c Release -- verify --dwarf`
      250 +
      251 +**232 of 254 replayable pairs pass (91%).** The remaining 22 are **the gate on removing AutoMapper** — the
      252 +migration is not finishable until each is resolved or consciously accepted.
      253 +
      254 +| Category | N | Meaning |
      255 +|---|---:|---|
      256 +| Not registered | 8 | Expected — see below |
      257 +| `ArgumentNullException` on a null source | 9 | **Genuine divergence** |
      258 +| Output differs from the golden master | 5 | **Genuine divergence** |
      259 +
      260 +### Not registered (8) — expected, no action beyond what is already recorded
      261 +
      262 +Three are the deliberate non-conversions (`BotPlatform → int`, `LiveStreamStatus → LiveStreamStatusDto`, and
      263 +`CommandOverviewDataDto → UserCommand`, which was backed out and needs the `internal`-constructor fix).
      264 +
      265 +The other five are **hand-written converters rather than `[GenerateMap]` pairs** —
      266 +`UserRanksDocument ↔ ICollection<Rank>`, `UserQuotesDocument ↔ ICollection<QuoteData>`, and
      267 +`HashSet<BotPlatform> ↔ AllowedOnPlatformFlag`. They are plain methods, so they never self-register into the
      268 +ambient registry, and the harness resolves only through it. **The code is fine; the harness cannot see it.**
      269 +Either expose them as registered pairs or exempt them explicitly in the manifest — do not "fix" the mappers.
      270 +
      271 +### `ArgumentNullException` (9) — the most important finding
      272 +
      273 +Generated maps open with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(source)`. Where a nested source member is null,
      274 +DwarfMapper **throws** while AutoMapper produced a null or empty result. Affected: the `AlertsLexicon`,
      275 +`UserLexicon`, `AlertsSettings`, `BotSettings` and `DiscordNotificationRequest` families — exactly the
      276 +graphs with abstract/dictionary-valued nested members.
      277 +
      278 +This is a **runtime-behaviour change on real data**, not a test artifact, and it is the same class of problem
      279 +as `NullStrategy` in §6: it builds clean and fails in production. Must be resolved before cutover.
      280 +
      281 +### Output differs (5)
      282 +
      283 +`NotificationTypeDto ↔ NotificationType`, `ViewsCounter → ViewsCounterDocument`,
      284 +`StreamerProfileData → StreamerProfileDataDocument`, `DbCommand → UserCommand`. The last is expected — it is
      285 +the documented `Identifier` loss through the `UserCommand.Empty` factory. The other four need diffing
      286 +member-by-member.

● Root cause confirmed — ObjectFactory.cs:165 returns null for any abstract or interface type:

  if (type.IsInterface || type.IsAbstract || depth >= MaxDepth)
      return type.IsValueType ? Activator.CreateInstance(type) : null;

  So the fuzzer can never exercise polymorphic graphs — the exact thing [MapDerivedType] exists to map. Let me fix the factory.

      22      }
      23
      24 +    /// <summary>
      25 +    ///     A concrete, parameterless-constructible type assignable to <paramref name="abstractType" />,
      26 +    ///     or <see langword="null" /> when the loaded assemblies offer none.
      27 +    /// </summary>
      28 +    /// <remarks>
      29 +    ///     Ordered by full name before the draw so the choice is a pure function of the seed — fixtures
      30 +    ///     must not shift because the runtime happened to enumerate assemblies differently.
      31 +    /// </remarks>
      32 +    private static Type? PickConcrete(Type abstractType, Random rng)
      33 +    {
      34 +        Type[] candidates;
      35 +        lock (ConcreteCandidates)
      36 +        {
      37 +            if (!ConcreteCandidates.TryGetValue(abstractType, out candidates!))
      38 +            {
      39 +                candidates = AppDomain.CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies()
      40 +                    .SelectMany(SafeTypes)
      41 +                    .Where(c => !c.IsAbstract && !c.IsInterface && !c.IsGenericTypeDefinition
      42 +                                && abstractType.IsAssignableFrom(c)
      43 +                                && c.GetConstructor(Type.EmptyTypes) is not null)
      44 +                    .OrderBy(c => c.FullName, StringComparer.Ordinal)
      45 +                    .ToArray();
      46 +                ConcreteCandidates[abstractType] = candidates;
      47 +            }
      48 +        }
      49 +
      50 +        return candidates.Length == 0 ? null : candidates[rng.Next(candidates.Length)];
      51 +    }
      52 +
      53 +    private static IEnumerable<Type> SafeTypes(Assembly assembly)
      54 +    {
      55 +        try
      56 +        {
      57 +            return assembly.GetTypes();
      58 +        }
      59 +        catch (ReflectionTypeLoadException ex)
      60 +        {
      61 +            // A half-loadable assembly must not take the whole scan down.
      62 +            return ex.Types.Where(t => t is not null)!;
      63 +        }
      64 +    }
      65 +
      66      /// <summary>Create a populated instance of <paramref name="type" /> using <paramref name="rng" />.</summary>
      67      public static object? Create(Type type, Random rng, int depth)
      68      {

      258
      260 -### Not registered (8) — expected, no action beyond what is already recorded
      259 +### The nine null failures were a fixture defect, not a migration defect
      260
      261 +The first run showed nine `ArgumentNullException`s across the `AlertsLexicon`, `UserLexicon`,
      262 +`AlertsSettings`, `BotSettings` and `DiscordNotificationRequest` families, and they looked like the most
      263 +serious finding of the migration: DwarfMapper throwing where AutoMapper returned null.
      264 +
      265 +They were not. `DwarfMapper.Testing.ObjectFactory` returned **null for any abstract or interface type**, so a
      266 +`Dictionary<BotPlatform, LexiconBaseDto>` — abstract value type — was fabricated with null *values*. Every
      267 +fixture built from it exercised the null path instead of the polymorphic dispatch path that
      268 +`[MapDerivedType]` exists to serve, and a mapper that correctly refuses nulls then looked like a regression.
      269 +
      270 +Fixed in the DwarfMapper repo: the factory now substitutes a concrete implementation, deterministically.
      271 +All nine disappeared, and six more pairs became capturable (284 vs 278).
      272 +
      273 +**Worth stating plainly:** a first attempt at this — switching to `ObjectFactoryV2` on the theory that it
      274 +respected nullability — made things *worse* (217/245) and was reverted on the measurement. The cause was
      275 +never nullability.
      276 +
      277 +### Not registered (8) — expected, no action against the mappers
      278 +
      279  Three are the deliberate non-conversions (`BotPlatform → int`, `LiveStreamStatus → LiveStreamStatusDto`, and
      263 -`CommandOverviewDataDto → UserCommand`, which was backed out and needs the `internal`-constructor fix).
      280 +`CommandOverviewDataDto → UserCommand`, backed out pending the `internal`-constructor fix).
      281
      282  The other five are **hand-written converters rather than `[GenerateMap]` pairs** —
      283  `UserRanksDocument ↔ ICollection<Rank>`, `UserQuotesDocument ↔ ICollection<QuoteData>`, and
      267 -`HashSet<BotPlatform> ↔ AllowedOnPlatformFlag`. They are plain methods, so they never self-register into the
      268 -ambient registry, and the harness resolves only through it. **The code is fine; the harness cannot see it.**
      269 -Either expose them as registered pairs or exempt them explicitly in the manifest — do not "fix" the mappers.
      284 +`HashSet<BotPlatform> ↔ AllowedOnPlatformFlag`. Plain methods never self-register into the ambient registry,
      285 +and the harness resolves only through it. **The code is fine; the harness cannot see it.** Expose them as

      35 +    /// <summary>Walks two JSON trees together and yields <c>path: expected -> actual</c> for each leaf that differs.</summary>
      36 +    private static IEnumerable<string> DiffTokens(JToken expected, JToken actual, string path = "$")
      37      {
      38 +        if (expected.Type != actual.Type)
      39 +        {
      40 +            yield return $"{path}: type {expected.Type} -> {actual.Type}";
      41 +            yield break;
      42 +        }
      43 +
      44 +        if (expected is JObject eo && actual is JObject ao)
      45 +        {
      46 +            foreach (var prop in eo.Properties())
      47 +            {
      48 +                var other = ao.Property(prop.Name, StringComparison.Ordinal);
      49 +                if (other is null)
      50 +                {
      51 +                    yield return $"{path}.{prop.Name}: present -> MISSING";
      52 +                    continue;
      53 +                }
      54 +
      55 +                foreach (var d in DiffTokens(prop.Value, other.Value, $"{path}.{prop.Name}"))
      56 +                {
      57 +                    yield return d;
      58 +                }
      59 +            }
      60 +
      61 +            foreach (var prop in ao.Properties())
      62 +                if (eo.Property(prop.Name, StringComparison.Ordinal) is null)
      63 +                    yield return $"{path}.{prop.Name}: MISSING -> present";
      64 +
      65 +            yield break;
      66 +        }
      67 +
      68 +        if (expected is JArray ea && actual is JArray aa)
      69 +        {
      70 +            if (ea.Count != aa.Count)
      71 +            {
      72 +                yield return $"{path}: length {ea.Count} -> {aa.Count}";
      73 +                yield break;
      74 +            }
      75 +
      76 +            for (var i = 0; i < ea.Count; i++)
      77 +                foreach (var d in DiffTokens(ea[i], aa[i], $"{path}[{i}]"))
      78 +                    yield return d;
      79 +
      80 +            yield break;
      81 +        }
      82 +
      83 +        if (!JToken.DeepEquals(expected, actual))
      84 +            yield return $"{path}: {expected} -> {actual}";
      85 +    }

● Found a serious DwarfMapper bug. The generated code for a top-level enum→enum pair is:

  public NotificationType Map(NotificationTypeDto src)
  {
      return new NotificationType
      {
      };          // ← src is ignored entirely; always returns default (0)
  }

  It treats the enum as an object to construct. As a member conversion it correctly uses __DwarfMap_EnumVal_…, but as a declared top-level pair it silently discards the source. Green build, no diagnostic,
  always returns 0. Let me pin it with a test.

● Remarkable finding. The source is PlatformsToShow: [4, 5] — two platforms. AutoMapper's output is [0]: one element, value zero, which isn't even in the source.

  AutoMapper couldn't convert HashSet<BotPlatform> → HashSet<int> element-wise and collapsed the whole set to {0}. DwarfMapper converts all of them correctly. So this isn't a regression — the migration
  fixes a live AutoMapper data-loss bug where users' platform selections were being persisted as {0} regardless of choice.

  Three DwarfMapper bugs found and fixed

  Your two nudges — "solution for abstracts" and "change it to internal" — each led directly to one:

  - ObjectFactory returned null for abstract/interface types, so polymorphic graphs were untestable and produced 9 phantom failures. Now substitutes a concrete implementation deterministically.

  The 8 "not registered" are harness visibility, not defects — five are hand-written collection converters that are plain methods and so never self-register.

  Three DwarfMapper bugs found and fixed

  Your two nudges — "solution for abstracts" and "change it to internal" — each led directly to one:

  - ObjectFactory returned null for abstract/interface types, so polymorphic graphs were untestable and produced 9 phantom failures. Now substitutes a concrete implementation deterministically.
  - A declared enum→enum pair emitted return new DstKind { }; — silently discarding the source and returning zero. Global mapped to User. Fixed by routing enum targets through the conversion path that
  collection pairs already use.
  - Use= converters were auto-adopted by unrelated members — would have written a date-prefixed document id into a plain donation-id field.

  6,109 tests pass across all four DwarfMapper suites.

  One correction worth stating: I tried ObjectFactoryV2 on a theory that the nulls were a nullability issue, measured it as worse (217/245), and reverted. The cause was abstract types, not nullability.
