● Real improvements across the board. Let me record the comparison and commit.

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\Docs\mapping-migration-ledger.md)
  ⎿  Added 46 lines
      316
      317  ---
      318
      319 +## Before / after
      320 +
      321 +Same machine (`DESKTOP-RULMB1S`, 12 logical cores, Windows 10.0.19045), Release, same harness binary.
      322 +**These figures are machine- and platform-dependent; do not quote them unqualified.**
      323 +
      324 +### Level A — the mapping subsystem in isolation
      325 +
      326 +| Metric | AutoMapper | DwarfMapper | |
      327 +|---|---:|---:|---|
      328 +| Phase 1 — config build | 899.5 ms | **253.8 ms** | 3.5× faster |
      329 +| Phase 1 — allocated | 10.31 MB | **0.20 MB** | 51× less |
      330 +| Phase 2 — first map of every pair | 436.5 ms | **78.5 ms** | 5.6× faster |
      331 +| Phase 2 — allocated | 22.39 MB | **0.23 MB** | 97× less |
      332 +| Managed heap delta | 9.46 MB | **0.24 MB** | 39× less |
      333 +| **Total mapper startup** | **1336 ms / 32.7 MB** | **332 ms / 0.43 MB** | **4× faster, 76× less allocated** |
      334 +
      335 +`AutoMapper` and `System.Reflection.Emit.Lightweight` no longer load at all — the runtime-compile
      336 +machinery is simply gone. That one is variance-free, unlike every timing above.
      337 +
      338 +*Caveat: the pair counts differ (298 vs 279), because a few pairs were deliberately not converted (§ the
      339 +"not registered" list). The per-pair figures are therefore indicative, not a like-for-like divisor. The
      340 +parity suite, not this benchmark, is what proves behaviour.*
      341 +
      342 +### Level B — the full Consumer service graph, out of process, N=10
      343 +
      344 +| Metric (median) | AutoMapper | DwarfMapper | |
      345 +|---|---:|---:|---|
      346 +| Process ready | 1956.5 ms | **1361.1 ms** | −30% |
      347 +| Provider build | 258.4 ms | **192.8 ms** | −25% |
      348 +| Graph realise total | 1823.1 ms | **1210.0 ms** | −34% |
      349 +| Working set | 113.9 MB | **97.2 MB** | −16.7 MB |
      350 +| Private memory | 39.4 MB | **29.4 MB** | −10.0 MB |
      351 +| CPU time | 1765.6 ms | **1257.8 ms** | −29% |
      352 +| Teardown | 22.8 ms | **15.5 ms** | −32% |
      353 +
      354 +The design predicted the mapper delta might disappear into Blazor/SignalR/OTel startup noise. It did not:
      355 +roughly a third off graph construction and 17 MB off working set, with IQRs tight enough (±3 ms on
      356 +readiness) that the difference is not noise.
      357 +
      358 +**One thing to check before shipping:** the graph now reports **1 unresolvable registration** where
      359 +AutoMapper's reported 0 (314 registrations, 301 resolved). That is one service that fails to construct on
      360 +this machine, and it appeared with the cutover. It is not covered by the parity suite — that tests
      361 +mapping, not DI — so it needs identifying.
      362 +
      363 +---
      364 +
      365  ## Open questions for the DwarfMapper repo
      366
      367  Raised by this migration; neither blocks it.

● Identified, and it's a genuine runtime break — not a benchmark artifact:

  IOmegaData :: DwarfMapMissingException: No map registered for
  List<Store> -> ICollection<StoreItem>

  StoreService.GetStoreAsync calls _mapper.Map<ICollection<StoreItem>>(data). AutoMapper derived collection maps implicitly from the element map; the ambient registry needs the collection pair declared.
  Notably the DWARF061 root couldn't catch this — the source type isn't statically known at that call site. Let me size how many such sites exist.

● PowerShell(cd C:\Users\Jouda\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution; $r = Get-ChildItem src -Recurse -Include *.cs,*.razor | Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\obj\\' } | Select-S…)

      355  roughly a third off graph construction and 17 MB off working set, with IQRs tight enough (±3 ms on

      357
      358 -**One thing to check before shipping:** the graph now reports **1 unresolvable registration** where
      359 -AutoMapper's reported 0 (314 registrations, 301 resolved). That is one service that fails to construct on
      360 -this machine, and it appeared with the cutover. It is not covered by the parity suite — that tests
      361 -mapping, not DI — so it needs identifying.
      358 +---
      359
      360 +## BLOCKING — ambient collection maps are not registered (47 call sites)
      361 +
      362 +**The branch builds, all 706 tests pass, and the parity suite is green on what it covers — but this is not
      363 +production-ready yet.** The service-graph benchmark caught it where nothing else did:
      364 +
      365 +```
      366 +IOmegaData :: DwarfMapMissingException: No DwarfMapper map is registered for
      367 +'List<Consumer.Database.Entities.Store>' -> 'ICollection<Consumer.Core.DataStructures.Models.Store.StoreItem>'
      368 +```
      369 +
      370 +`StoreService.GetStoreAsync` does `_mapper.Map<ICollection<StoreItem>>(data)` where `data` is
      371 +`List<DbStore>`. **AutoMapper derived collection maps implicitly from the element map. DwarfMapper's ambient
      372 +registry does not** — it resolves an exact `(source, target)` pair, and no collection pair is declared.
      373 +
      374 +**There are 47 such call sites** across the API controllers, services and Razor components
      375 +(`Map<ICollection<T>>`, `Map<List<T>>`, …). Each throws `DwarfMapMissingException` on first use.
      376 +
      377 +### Why nothing else caught it
      378 +
      379 +- **The 706 tests** construct services directly or mock them; they never resolve this path through DI.
      380 +- **The parity suite** replays *declared* pairs. Ambient collection resolution is not a declared pair.
      381 +- **`DWARF061` (the validation root) cannot see it.** The check needs the source type, and at
      382 +  `Map<ICollection<StoreItem>>(data)` only the *destination* is static — the source is whatever `data`
      383 +  happens to be. So the build passes.
      384 +
      385 +That combination is worth remembering: three independent safety nets, none of which covers this shape.
      386 +
      387 +### The fix, in order of preference
      388 +
      389 +1. **Declare the collection pairs.** `[GenerateMap<List<DbStore>, ICollection<StoreItem>>]` per used
      390 +   instantiation — DwarfMapper already routes top-level collection pairs through the collection converter,
      391 +   so the element map is reused and nothing is hand-written. Mechanical, ~47 pairs, and it keeps the call
      392 +   sites untouched.
      393 +2. **Inject the concrete mapper** at those call sites instead of the facade. Fewer declarations, but it
      394 +   changes 47 call sites and loses the cross-assembly ergonomics the facade was chosen for.
      395 +3. **Upstream:** have `DwarfMapperRegistry` synthesise a collection map from a registered element map on
      396 +   miss. That is what AutoMapper did, it would fix all 47 at once, and it is the only option that stops the
      397 +   next consumer hitting the same wall. Worth raising against DwarfMapper.
      398 +
      399 +**Do not ship the branch until this is resolved.**
      400 +
      401  ---

● All nine tasks are done and both trees are clean. But the final verification found something that changes the bottom line, so let me lead with that.

  The branch is not shippable yet — one blocking defect

  The service-graph benchmark caught what three other safety nets missed:

  IOmegaData :: DwarfMapMissingException: no map registered for
  List<Store> -> ICollection<StoreItem>

  StoreService.GetStoreAsync calls _mapper.Map<ICollection<StoreItem>>(data). AutoMapper derived collection maps implicitly from the element map; DwarfMapper's ambient registry resolves an exact (source,
  target) pair. There are 47 such call sites, each of which would throw on first use.

  Worth knowing why nothing else caught it:
  - The 706 tests construct services directly or mock them — they never resolve that path through DI.
  - The parity suite replays declared pairs; ambient collection resolution isn't one.
  - DWARF061 can't see it: at Map<ICollection<T>>(data) only the destination is static, so the build passes.

  Fix options are in the ledger; the cleanest is declaring the collection pairs (DwarfMapper already routes top-level collection pairs through the collection converter, so element maps are reused). The
  best long-term fix is upstream — have the registry synthesise a collection map from a registered element map.

  What is done

  All 12 profiles converted, AutoMapper fully removed, 706 tests pass, solution builds at 0 errors including the DWARF061 validation root.

  ┌───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────┐

  The fix shape is already proven: adding one [GenerateMap<List<DbStore>, ICollection<StoreItem>>] took the service graph from 1 unresolvable → 0, and the generator routes top-level collection pairs
  through the collection converter, so the element maps are reused rather than rewritten.

  Worth being precise about why runtime synthesis isn't the answer here: DwarfMapper could have made these work automatically the way AutoMapper did, but only via reflection — which is exactly the AOT and
  trim guarantee the library exists to provide. Declaring each used instantiation is the cost of that guarantee. That tradeoff is the migration in miniature.

  I'll verify with a full build, the 706-test suite, and the graph check reaching 0 unresolvable once they land.

● The Services agent uncovered something structural that reframes this fix. Two independent gates exist per call site:

  - Compile time — DWARF061 checks the static argument type.
  - Run time — DwarfMapperRegistry.Map looks up by source.GetType() and walks base types only, no interfaces.

  So where a method is declared ICollection<T> but returns List<T>, both pairs are needed — one satisfies the build, the other satisfies the lookup. That's why my single-pair proof made the graph go green
  while 46 sites stayed broken.

  And one site can't be fixed by declaration at all: document.LivestreamStatistics.TakeLast(count).SelectMany(x => x.Value) has a runtime type that is a private System.Linq.Enumerable iterator — an
  undeclarable type.

  That's a genuine structural limit of the ambient facade for collection sources, not a gap in anyone's diligence. The remedies are per-site: materialise with .ToList() before mapping, or call the
  concrete mapper (statically typed, so neither gate applies). I'll decide that once the other two agents report.

      849                          into.Add(s);
      850          }
      851
      852 +        // [MapConstructor<S,T>("Factory")] names the factory as a CONSTRUCTOR argument, not a named one,
      853 +        // so the Use= scan above cannot see it. It must be reserved for the same reason and with more
      854 +        // force: a factory only CONSTRUCTS its pair's target, after which the pair assigns members.
      855 +        // Adopted as a general element converter it produces an object with nothing filled in — the
      856 +        // collection case that surfaced this returned an Empty singleton, so a whole collection mapped
      857 +        // to blanks, silently and with a green build.
      858 +        static void ScanCtorFactories(HashSet<string> into, System.Collections.Immutable.ImmutableArray<AttributeData> attrs)
      859 +        {
      860 +            foreach (var a in attrs)
      861 +            {
      862 +                if (a.AttributeClass?.Name is not (KnownNames.MapConstructor or KnownNames.MapConstructor + "Attribute"))
      863 +                    continue;
      864 +
      865 +                foreach (var ca in a.ConstructorArguments)
      866 +                    if (ca.Value is string s && !string.IsNullOrEmpty(s))
      867 +                        into.Add(s);
      868 +            }
      869 +        }
      870 +
      871          Scan(reserved, classSymbol.GetAttributes());
      872 +        ScanCtorFactories(reserved, classSymbol.GetAttributes());
      873          foreach (var member in classSymbol.GetMembers())
      874 +        {
      875              Scan(reserved, member.GetAttributes());
      876 +            ScanCtorFactories(reserved, member.GetAttributes());
      877 +        }
      878
      879          return reserved;
      880      }

● DWARF008 — the factory owns construction on the declared pair, so "number" isn't a valid target there. I've hit a genuine DwarfMapper limitation: a collection pair whose element pair is a class-level
  [GenerateMap] with a factory synthesizes a fresh element mapper instead of reusing the declared one, and the synthesized one can't construct. Reverting the quotes attempt rather than leaving a broken
  build.

      261 +// NOT DONE — quotes. QuotesRepository:34 and :43 still throw at runtime.
      262 +//
      263 +// The obvious fix (map the inner collection, and declare
      264 +// `ICollection<QuoteDocument> <-> ICollection<QuoteData>` here) does not work, and the reason is a
      265 +// DwarfMapper limitation worth knowing:
      266 +//
      267 +//   A collection pair resolves its ELEMENT converter against declared partial METHODS. A class-level
      268 +//   [GenerateMap] pair is not one, so the collection pair SYNTHESIZES a fresh element mapper — which
      269 +//   cannot see the pair's [MapConstructor] factory. QuoteData has no parameterless constructor, so the
      270 +//   synthesized mapper fails DWARF024 on the `number` parameter. Binding that parameter on the declared
      271 +//   pair is rejected in turn (DWARF008), because there the factory owns construction.
      272 +//
      273 +// So the element pair is simultaneously "has a factory" (declared) and "must construct itself"
      274 +// (synthesized), and no attribute satisfies both. Options: give QuoteData a parameterless constructor,
      275 +// declare the element pair as a partial METHOD so the collection pair can reuse it, or have
      276 +// QuotesRepository take the concrete mapper (which needs a project reference it does not have).
      277 +// Recorded in Docs/mapping-migration-ledger.md.
      278  [GenerateMap<DiscordNotificationDocument, DiscordNotification>]
      279  [GenerateMap<DiscordEmbedFieldDocument, DiscordEmbedField>]

  ⎿  Added 52 lines, removed 1 line
      396     miss. That is what AutoMapper did, it would fix all 47 at once, and it is the only option that stops the
      397     next consumer hitting the same wall. Worth raising against DwarfMapper.
      398
      399 -**Do not ship the branch until this is resolved.**
      399 +### Resolved — 45 of 47, plus 5 more the original scan missed
      400
      401 +Declared **~40 collection pairs** across `DtosMappers`, `ChattersMappers`, `RepositoryMappers`,
      402 +`ServicesMappers` and `ChatHistoryMappers`, and materialised 5 lazy LINQ sources with `.ToList()`.
      403 +Runtime graph check: **0 unresolvable**. Full solution builds clean; **706 tests pass**.
      404 +
      405 +Three things learned doing it, each of which cost a false start:
      406 +
      407 +**1. Two keys per call site, and they are often different types.** `IDwarfMapper.Map<TDest>(object)` has
      408 +two independent gates: `DWARF061` validates the call site's **static** argument type at build, while
      409 +`DwarfMapperRegistry.Map` looks the delegate up by `source.GetType()` and walks **base types only —
      410 +never interfaces**. So a method declared `Task<ICollection<T>>` that returns the repository's `List<T>`
      411 +needs **both** pairs: the interface-keyed one to build, the `List<T>`-keyed one to actually resolve.
      412 +Declaring only the static type compiles and still throws.
      413 +
      414 +**2. A lazy LINQ source can never be declared.** `Where(...)`/`SelectMany(...)` return private
      415 +`System.Linq` iterator types. No attribute can name them and they do not derive from `IEnumerable<T>` —
      416 +they implement it. Five call sites therefore needed `.ToList()`; that call is load-bearing, not style,
      417 +and each is commented as such.
      418 +
      419 +**3. Declare a collection pair beside its ELEMENT pair, not beside its call site.** `Consumer.Repositories`
      420 +does not reference `Services` or `Omega`, so a pair declared there could see the *types* but not the
      421 +element pair's *configuration* — the generator would synthesise a fresh convention-only element map and
      422 +fail completeness. The ambient registry is process-wide, so declaring beside the element map resolves
      423 +the far-away call site anyway.
      424 +
      425 +### Still open (2 call sites) — `QuotesRepository:34` and `:43`
      426 +
      427 +`UserQuotesDocument ↔ ICollection<QuoteData>` is object↔collection, which is not a DwarfMapper shape.
      428 +Mapping the inner collection instead (what AutoMapper's `ConstructUsing` did literally) runs into a
      429 +genuine limitation:
      430 +
      431 +> A collection pair resolves its ELEMENT converter against declared partial **methods**. A class-level
      432 +> `[GenerateMap]` pair is not one, so the collection pair **synthesises** a fresh element mapper — which
      433 +> cannot see the pair's `[MapConstructor]` factory. `QuoteData` has no parameterless constructor, so the
      434 +> synthesised mapper fails `DWARF024` on the `number` parameter. Binding that parameter on the declared
      435 +> pair is rejected in turn (`DWARF008`), because there the factory owns construction.
      436 +
      437 +The element pair must be simultaneously "has a factory" and "must construct itself", and no attribute
      438 +satisfies both. Options: give `QuoteData` a parameterless constructor; declare the element pair as a
      439 +partial **method** so the collection pair can reuse it; or have `QuotesRepository` take the concrete
      440 +mapper (needs a project reference it does not have). **Worth raising upstream** — a collection pair
      441 +should reuse a declared `[GenerateMap]` element pair rather than synthesising past it.
      442 +
      443 +### Known behavioural divergence — polymorphic collection elements
      444 +
      445 +`List<Command> → ICollection<CommandOverviewDataDto>` dispatches through the element pair at **compile**
      446 +time, so an `AliasCommand` in the list is mapped as a plain `Command` and loses `Alias`/`IsAlias`.
      447 +AutoMapper dispatched on the **runtime** type. The fix is `[MapDerivedType]` arms on the element pair —
      448 +a behaviour change, so it is recorded rather than made.
      449 +
      450 +**Do not ship until the two Quotes sites and the polymorphic dispatch are resolved.**
      451 +
      452  ---

  ~40 collection pairs declared, 5 lazy LINQ sources materialised. Three findings, each of which cost a false start:

  Two keys per call site. DWARF061 validates the static argument type at build; the registry resolves by source.GetType() and walks base types only, never interfaces. A method declared ICollection<T>
  returning List<T> needs both pairs. That's why my first single-pair fix turned the graph green while 46 sites stayed broken — I'd proved the wrong thing.

  A lazy LINQ source can never be declared. Where/SelectMany return private iterators no attribute can name. Five sites needed .ToList() — load-bearing, and commented as such.

  Declare beside the element pair, not the call site. The Repositories agent proved this by refusing the task and showing the build errors: declaring there sees the types but not the element pair's
  config, so the generator synthesises a convention-only map that fails completeness.

  Two sites remain open — deliberately not bodged

  QuotesRepository:34 and :43 hit a real DwarfMapper limitation: a collection pair synthesises its element mapper rather than reusing a declared class-level [GenerateMap] pair, so it can't see that pair's
  [MapConstructor] factory (DWARF024) — while binding the constructor parameter on the declared pair is rejected because there the factory owns construction (DWARF008). The element pair must be both "has
  a factory" and "must construct itself". I reverted my attempt rather than leave a broken build.

  Also recorded: List<Command> elements dispatch at compile time, so an AliasCommand in a list loses Alias where AutoMapper dispatched on runtime type. Fixing it needs [MapDerivedType] arms — a behaviour
  change, so it's recorded, not made.

  Final state

  22 commits on mapper-to-dwarf-transition, 4 on DwarfMapper's fix branch. Nothing pushed — both await your review.

  The four DwarfMapper fixes, all with regression tests and 6,111 tests green:
  Use= converters leaking to unrelated members · a [MapConstructor] factory adopted as a collection element converter (whole collections mapping to blanks) · declared enum pairs silently returning zero ·
  ObjectFactory returning null for abstract types.

  Three of those four were silent-data-loss bugs that a green build would have shipped.

      620 +    // what is needed and what Use= forbids (DWARF014). One member, one branch, no structural change.
      621 +    [MapIgnore(nameof(CommandOverviewDataDto.Alias))]           // filled by FillAliasIfAliasCommand

      627 +    /// <summary>
      628 +    ///     Copies <see cref="AliasCommand.Alias" /> when the runtime type is an alias.
      629 +    /// </summary>
      630 +    /// <remarks>
      631 +    ///     This is the runtime type test AutoMapper performed by dispatching on <c>GetType()</c>. A plain
      632 +    ///     <c>Command</c> has no alias, so the member correctly stays null for every other subtype.
      633 +    /// </remarks>
      634 +    private static void FillAliasIfAliasCommand(Command source, CommandOverviewDataDto target)
      635 +    {
      636 +        if (source is AliasCommand alias)
      637 +        {
      638 +            target.Alias = alias.Alias;
      639 +        }
      640 +    }
      641 +

● Update(~\RiderProjects\ConsumerSolution\Docs\mapping-migration-ledger.md)
  ⎿  Added 44 lines, removed 1 line
      422  fail completeness. The ambient registry is process-wide, so declaring beside the element map resolves
      423  the far-away call site anyway.
      424
      425 -### Still open (2 call sites) — `QuotesRepository:34` and `:43`
      425 +### RESOLVED — `QuotesRepository:34` and `:43`
      426
      427 +Fixed by **replacing the `[MapConstructor]` factory with constructor-PARAMETER binding** on the element
      428 +pair, and mapping the inner collection at the call sites.
      429 +
      430 +The factory was the whole obstacle. A collection pair synthesises its own element mapper, and a
      431 +synthesised mapper cannot see a factory (`DWARF024` on the unbound parameter) — while binding the
      432 +parameter on a factory-bearing pair is rejected in turn (`DWARF008`, because there the factory owns
      433 +construction). Parameter binding satisfies **both** the declared pair and any synthesised one:
      434 +
      435 +```csharp
      436 +[GenerateMap<QuoteDocument, QuoteData>]
      437 +[MapProperty<QuoteDocument, QuoteData>(nameof(QuoteDocument.Index), "number")]   // ctor parameter
      438 +```
      439 +
      440 +Verified in the emitted code: `new QuoteData(...)` appears in both the declared pair and the synthesised
      441 +element mapper. The call sites now map `data.Quotes` rather than the document, which is literally what
      442 +AutoMapper's `ConstructUsing(x => ctx.Mapper.Map<ICollection<QuoteData>>(x.Quotes))` did.
      443 +
      444 +**Generalisable lesson: prefer constructor-parameter binding over a `[MapConstructor]` factory** unless
      445 +the construction genuinely cannot be expressed as parameter mapping. A factory is invisible to
      446 +synthesised mappers, and it also blocks `init`-only members (which is why `UserCommand.Identifier` is
      447 +still lost — same root cause, recorded above).
      448 +
      449 +### RESOLVED — polymorphic `Command` elements lost `Alias`
      450 +
      451 +A collection loop over `List<Command>` binds the base pair at **compile** time, so an `AliasCommand` in
      452 +the list was mapped as a plain `Command` and silently dropped its `Alias` — visible in the API response,
      453 +since `GetUserCommandsAsync` returns exactly such a mixed list.
      454 +
      455 +`[MapDerivedType<AliasCommand, CommandOverviewDataDto>]` is the textbook fix and **does not work here**:
      456 +the arm must resolve to a declared partial overload (`DWARF035` otherwise), and adding one makes the
      457 +reverse `CommandOverviewDataDto → AliasCommand` direction ambiguous (`DWARF013`), because that pair
      458 +already owns a method. Renaming to an overload did not help.
      459 +
      460 +Fixed with an `[AfterMap]` hook that performs the runtime type test directly. `[AfterMap]` receives the
      461 +whole source — exactly what is needed and what `Use=` forbids (`DWARF014`) — so one member and one
      462 +branch restore the behaviour with no structural change. Verified in the emitted code:
      463 +`FillAliasIfAliasCommand(source, __dwarf_target);`.
      464 +
      465 +*(`[AfterMap]` decorates the hook itself and is wired by SIGNATURE; it is not named from the mapping
      466 +method, which `AfterMapAttribute` has no constructor for.)*
      467 +
      468 +### Historic — the analysis that led there
      469 +
      470  `UserQuotesDocument ↔ ICollection<QuoteData>` is object↔collection, which is not a DwarfMapper shape.
      471  Mapping the inner collection instead (what AutoMapper's `ConstructUsing` did literally) runs into a
      472  genuine limitation:

● Both blockers are closed. The migration is complete.

  The last two gaps

  Quotes — the [MapConstructor] factory was the obstacle, not an inherent limitation. A collection pair synthesises its own element mapper, which can't see a factory (DWARF024), while binding the
  constructor parameter on a factory-bearing pair is rejected because there the factory owns construction (DWARF008). Constructor-parameter binding satisfies both:

  [MapProperty<QuoteDocument, QuoteData>(nameof(QuoteDocument.Index), "number")]

  That generalises: prefer parameter binding over a factory. A factory is invisible to synthesised mappers and blocks init-only members — which is precisely why UserCommand.Identifier is still lost. Same
  root cause, twice.

  Polymorphic Command elements — AliasCommand in a List<Command> was silently losing its Alias in API responses. [MapDerivedType] is the textbook fix and genuinely doesn't work here: the arm needs a
  declared partial overload (DWARF035), and adding one makes the reverse direction ambiguous (DWARF013). An [AfterMap] hook does it in one branch — it receives the whole source, which is exactly what Use=
  forbids.

  Both verified in emitted code, not inferred from a green build.
